# Narvi — Implementation plan, broken into Steps

## Context

Narvi's technical specification (autonomous coding agents in sandboxes) is in
[docs/TECHNICAL_PLAN.md](TECHNICAL_PLAN.md) (§0-§45), and the nine-view UI design spec is in
[docs/design/mockups.html](design/mockups.html). This plan breaks 22 phases (0-21) into **229 Steps**, counted from the tables below.
172 of them are scheduled work in this repository (Steps 108-110 were routed out to a separate
repository when the extension boundary was drawn, and keep their numbers as pointers so every
citation of them stays valid) — including Phase 4's own 5 additive Steps, 40-44, Phase 8's 9
shadow-mode Steps, 96-104, Phase 9's 6 knowledge Steps, 105-110, and Phase 12's 4 boundary Steps,
132-135, Phase 13's 7 silent-failure Steps, 136-142, Phase 14's 5 composition Steps, 143-147,
Phase 15's 4 guardrail Steps, 148-151, Phase 16's 8 external-client and MCP Steps, 157-159,
180-183 and 217 (158 and 159 still wait on the separate, still-open native-client decision), Phase 17's 4 parity Steps, 162-165, Phase 18's 5 Kubernetes-provider Steps, 166-170,
Phase 19's 5 adopted-capability Steps, 184-188, Phase 20's 8 GitHub App pool Steps, 190-197, and Phase 21's 10 adopted Steps, 206-215. The other 57 are Phase 11 (Steps 113-131, plus 152-156, 160-161, 171, 172-179, 189, 198-205, 216 and 218-229 appended after the phase was scheduled): named
gaps, each filed as the shipping Step
that found it declared it, and since scheduled in full with an execution order and a milestone of
its own. They gate nothing else, which is why Phase 12 follows them numerically without waiting
on them; the one ordering a row stated on Steps outside the phase (group H's, for a new session
timer kind) was an either/or whose other branch needed no Phase 11 row at all, and the row it named,
222, has shipped, so it gates nothing either. Each Step is individually shippable and CI-green,
executable by a developer assisted by coding agents (Sonnet 5).
Every Step references the technical-plan section that specifies it. Each Step becomes exactly one PR when
implemented — but a Step's number (e.g. Step 6) is the plan's own row number, not the GitHub PR number it
becomes; the two are unrelated counters that will drift apart (e.g. a docs-only or hotfix PR consumes a GitHub
PR number without consuming a Step), so never write "PR-06" expecting it to mean GitHub PR #6.

**Assumptions** (challenge if needed): mono-module Go, trunk-based (small PRs to `main`, feature flags for
incomplete paths). The mockups ([docs/design/mockups.html](design/mockups.html)) and the §6 contracts are the
spec of record for behavior. Note the mockups do not cover every individual screen phase 7 will need — a screen
genuinely required but absent from the artifact must be derived from the same design system (docs/
TECHNICAL_PLAN.md §12), never invented independently of it.

## Cross-cutting conventions (apply to every Step)

- One Step = one PR: one coherent scope + its tests; `go test -race` and lint green; no naked goroutines; no timeout literal outside `platform/` (lint-check from Step 2).
- Every state write = one transaction (transition + event + outbox) once the infra exists (Step 11).
- Steps marked ∥ can run in parallel (independent control-plane / sandbox-agent / UI streams).
- Every phase end = a demonstrable milestone (the exit criterion from technical plan §10).

---

|---|---|---|---|

| 2 | platform: config & timeouts | Typed config validated at boot (fail-fast, named errors); `platform/timeouts.go`: single hierarchy + **invariant test** (provider cap > supervisor > bridge > SSE); lint-check forbidding `time.Second*N` elsewhere | §5.4 |
| 3 | platform: observability | `slog` envelope (correlation_id, session_id, sandbox_gen), correlation-id middleware, baseline OTel traces/metrics | §5.3 |
| 4 | postgres: core schema | Migrations: `users`, `identities`, `sessions`, `turns`, `sandboxes` (UNIQUE(session_id)) + `sandbox_history`, `events`, `session_timers`, `outbox`, `participants`, `artifacts`, `audit_log`; sqlc + store skeletons | §2, §5.1, §13.2 |
| 5 | contracts: schemas + codegen | JSON Schemas: sandbox WS, client WS, SESSION_CONFIG, REST DTOs; Go + TS codegen; round-trip tests (the `tokens` **object**-not-number case — dedicated test) | §6 |
| 6 | dev-loop & internal auth | HMAC helpers (3 distinct secrets per direction), `/health`, `make dev` (Postgres compose), `narvi serve` skeleton | §5.2 |

**Phase 0 milestone**: `make dev` boots, contracts round-trip green.

## Phase 4 — Warm boot & agent-turn resilience (5 Steps, additive)

Sequentially-numbered Steps, placed here rather than appended at the very end of the plan because
they gate nothing in Phase 3 and don't need to wait for Phase 5 to be scheduled. Standing rule this
plan follows whenever new Steps are inserted mid-sequence rather than appended: every following Step
number shifts to make room, so a Step's number identifies its position in the CURRENT plan, never a
fixed historical slot — nothing depends on a Step keeping the number it once had.
That premise rests on discipline, and **partly** on enforcement — a distinction
this paragraph previously erased, and the phase audit caught. `internal/ops`'s own
`TestNoStepRefInSource` really does keep Step numbers out of source, but it scans
`internal/`, `cmd/`, `contracts/` and `web/src` only. **183 Step citations live
outside that scan**: 155 in `migrations/`, plus `docs/runbooks`, `docs/guides`,
`deploy/` and `.github/`. A renumber touches those too, and nothing tells you so.

What made every Phase 7 insertion (three backend Steps added mid-phase, once the
screens above them turned out to have nothing to read) a cheap change was that
the shifted range happened not to be cited in those places — not that it could
not have been. Closing this is a real sweep and is filed as such, because the
only honest way to do it is to check each citation against the row it names:
arithmetic was tried on the technical plan's own citations during this phase and
got five of them wrong, three of which had already been broken by an earlier
renumber in a way no shift could repair.

The range those insertions shifted is deliberately not written here: a sentence
about the mechanism should not itself need renumbering.

| Step | Title | Content | Ref. |
|---|---|---|---|
| 40 | warm boot: fetch-aware git sync | `sandbox-agent/gitclone.SyncAll` gains a bounded `git fetch` step before checkout (remote-tracking preferred over the local-HEAD fallback) + credential-helper wiring; explicit-branch-fetch-failure hard rule (never silently fork at a stale base); new `gitstate` fetch states/triggers + table-driven tests | §19.3, §3.4 |
| 41 | warm boot: shared image fingerprint | Redefine `imagebuild.Fingerprint` to key on repo clone URLs, not SHAs (one shared image per repo set); `ports.ImageSpec` → `{Base, Repos{URL,SHA}, RuntimeVersion}`; baked `/narvi/image-manifest.json`; `image_builds` migration (`built_repo_shas`, `built_at`); spawn path drops the per-repo `ResolveBranchSHA` loop | §19.1, §4.1 |
| 42 | warm boot: refresh pump + hook policy | `imagebuild.Builder` freshness pump (poll default-branch tips, in-place `image_ref` swap, never degrades availability) + platform GitHub credential; `EvaluateHook`'s `workspaceMoved` policy for `repo_image` (**§6.4 breaking-change amendment**, non-fatal rerun); bounded ANSI-stripped hook-output-tail capture; per-hook rerun-duration telemetry; `sparse-checkout disable` hardening for the unscoped `snapshot_restore` case; new §9.3-class scenarios (fetch-fail/stale-image/refresh-in-flight/non-idempotent-setup boots) | §19.2, §19.4, §19.5, §6.4 |
| 43 | warm boot: dependency-work reduction (3 PRs — (a)+(b)+(c) all shipped) | Three deliverables answering one question — the dependency work a warm boot and a build each redo is expensive and fragile. **None is telemetry-gated**: earlier drafts held (b) behind rerun-duration telemetry and (c) behind build failure rate, and §19.6/§19.9 record why neither gate survived scrutiny — each measured something that could not resolve its tier's actual risk. **(a) and (b) shipped together, in one PR**; **(c) shipped as its own, later PR**, sequenced last for size, not held for a number. **(a) Skip the rerun outright** when a dependency-manifest (lockfile) digest baked beside `built_repo_shas` in `/narvi/image-manifest.json` still matches the checked-out tree — this extends the Step 41 manifest with a new `dependency_manifest_digests` field; this captures the common case `workspaceMoved`'s SHA inequality cannot distinguish — code moved, dependencies did not — and it stays **non-fatal on failure for availability reasons, not because the predicate proves nothing** (§19.6 records why that inference must not be drawn). **Ungated** — (a) avoids provably unnecessary work rather than making slow work faster, and trades away no correctness (it skips only on proof and falls through on any doubt), so it does not wait on the §19.5 histogram. **(b) Delta script** — optional repo-authored `sync.sh` runs instead of full `setup.sh` when only dependencies drifted (`git diff --quiet <built_sha> HEAD -- setup.sh` against the baked manifest); soft-fails to full `setup.sh`, then warn-and-continue (never fatal); every ladder decision logs a structured reason. **Ungated too, shipped with (a)** — the rerun-duration gate (§19.5) only ever answered half of (b)'s question: it measures whether reruns are slow, never whether repo owners will author a delta script, which is the likelier way this tier fails. And (b) concedes almost nothing either way — purely additive and opt-in (a repo with no delta script keeps today's behavior byte for byte), strictly conservative eligibility, every failure path falling back to full `setup.sh` then warn-and-continue. §19.5's telemetry becomes the post-ship confirmation and the tuning trigger, not the precondition. **(c) Build-time dependency cache — shipped as its own PR, now on its third iteration: immutable versioned cache snapshots.** A build no longer mounts a shared, mutable volume at all — every successful build publishes a brand-new, immutable, distinctly-numbered version under a cache key (`domain/imagebuild.CacheVolumeKey(base, runtimeVersion)` — no rotation epoch anymore, repos excluded by construction, same as before), and a build mounts exactly ONE already-published version read-only (`ports.CacheMount.MountVersion`), publishing a DIFFERENT new one (`PublishVersion`) if it succeeds — never the same identifier. `ports.BuildOutcome.PublishedCacheVersion` (BuildImage's new success-path return value) confirms whether an eventual successful call genuinely still carried the mount, so `app/imagebuild.Builder` never records a publication that never happened. Landing only on `internal/adapters/outbound/modal`, the one adapter with `Capabilities().ImageBuilds` true — RWX unaffected, its own content-addressed layer cache already provides this effect natively (§4.1.1). **Concurrency: two earlier attempts got this wrong, and the plan record says so rather than silently replacing them.** Attempt 1 mounted one shared volume read-write on the false premise that every well-known cache path is content-addressed (checked against each package manager's own real layout and found false for nearly every path — Go's own module cache ships a `cache/lock` file for exactly this reason, Gradle's dependency cache is guarded by a lock living inside the mounted directory, npm/pip key their caches by request URL and mutate/append in place — §19.1's own closing paragraphs have the full per-tool findings). Attempt 2 mounted that same shared volume read-only for a build's duration with exactly one write-back after success, reasoning that this made a lock unnecessary — but the write-back was ITSELF an unguarded writer into state a concurrent reader could observe, the identical hazard in smaller form, and this was caught in review rather than shipped as correct. This (third) iteration removes the write window instead of narrowing it further: under an immutable-version model a lock is not merely unneeded, it is MEANINGLESS — there is no shared mutable state left for one to guard. Rotation (attempt 2's own `platform.Config.CacheVolumeEpoch`/`NARVI_CACHE_VOLUME_EPOCH`) is gone, not merely unused: a bad published version is escaped by pointing a later build at an earlier, known-good one (an operator removing its row from this control plane's own version-history bookkeeping), which falls out of the version-history model for free rather than needing a second, parallel rotation config surface. `internal/adapters/outbound/modal`'s own `BuildImage` still declines and retries cold the instant its own wire protocol reports cache trouble (corruption, unavailability, a build-service-reported internal timeout, or a not-found/pruned version) — narrowed from the prior design to never treat a bare CLIENT-side transport timeout as cache trouble (that broadening meant a build legitimately exceeding `ProviderHTTPClientTimeout` for reasons unrelated to the cache retried cold against the identical budget, doubling wall clock and very likely failing twice instead of succeeding once). **Retention is specified, not left implicit**: `domain/imagebuild.RetainedCacheVersions` (5) and `PruneCacheVersions` keep the newest N confirmed versions per cache key and prune this control plane's own bookkeeping beyond that immediately after every confirmed publish — pruning a bookkeeping row is always safe against an already-in-flight build that resolved it earlier (it never retroactively invalidates an already-sent request); reclaiming the underlying provider-side bytes for a pruned version remains a named, deferred build-service obligation, not something this repository's own Go code does. **Cross-tenant provenance is unchanged and stated plainly**: one cache key is still shared fleet-wide across every repo set on the same base/runtime, so a published version's bytes were still produced by running some other repo's own `setup.sh` — versioning fixes WHEN a version is safe to read, never WHOSE code produced it. Build-duration/failure-rate instrumentation (`image_build_duration_seconds`, `image_build_attempt_total`, tagged by phase/outcome) shipped alongside it, ungated, to size the win and catch a regression | §19.1, §19.6 |
| 44 ∥ | opencode: context-overflow compaction retry | Adapter-local classification on the already-decoded `ContextOverflowError` tagged union; one forced-compaction retry per turn via `POST /session/{id}/summarize` (new `OpenCodeSummarizeTimeout`), entirely inside one `StartTurn` call; no new `FailureReason`; CI contract test pins `/summarize`'s availability on the pinned OpenCode binary | §7.2 |

**Phase 4 milestone**: warm-boot staleness window observed within §19.2's predicted 10–40 min range; the new §9.3-class scenarios green; compaction-retry contract test green. Independent of Phase 3's own milestone; Phase 5 Steps may proceed regardless of this phase's status.

## Phase 5 — Code review & automations (27 Steps)

| Step | Title | Content | Ref. |
|---|---|---|---|
| 45 | domain/review | Risk-map verdict, severities, **verdict floor in the domain**; verdict is a first-class structured type from day one — `RiskLevel`, `PremiseState` (`ok/questionable/not_a_pr`), `BlastRadius []Tag` (fixed vocabulary), `FilesChanged int`, `TestsCoverageState`, `DocsDriftState`, `Shippable` (`auto/needs_human/block`, server-computed only, never the model's self-report) — never re-derived by parsing a posted comment; two independent raise-only floors (coverage, premise) compose via `max(rank)`, exactly one exported pure function per floor and one for `Shippable` | §8.2 |
| 46 | review sessions | Per-PR reuse, atomic claim on concurrent mentions, re-trigger via label/button (any re-run phrasing recommended to a user must be routable by the classifier's deterministic fallback too, §5.2) — or automatically on new commits, a second and independent trigger path (§24) — inline pre-fetched diff; a PR's inline-pre-fetched context additionally carries GitHub's own stack position/size/ultimate base when present (**amendment**, §17.6) — consumed as review context only, never as additional diff to verdict over (§21.1's stacked-PR review-scope decision) | §8.2, §5.2, §21.1 |
| 47 | server-side verdict | Verdict-posting tool + raw-comment blocking (scoped to review sessions), `review:*` label sync, formal-review gate; the posting tool's payload is the structured `domain/review` verdict (Step 45) — the agent calls it with typed fields, never free text parsed back out of markdown, including any re-run phrasing it recommends to a user (rendered server-side from those same typed fields, never reproduced by a model, and routable by the classifier's deterministic fallback, §5.2); `blockOnHighRisk` (admin, per-repo, strict-boolean) reuses the same formal-review submission path and carries no independent permission of its own; **auto-approval is criteria-driven, never label-triggered** — the server recomputes `Shippable` deterministically and checks it against CI-green/no-floor-raised/diff-size/sensitive-path criteria (the eligibility engine itself ships in Step 62); the existing `review: low risk` label **inverts** into a `review: needs-human` escape hatch that forces human review on a PR regardless of criteria — a deliberate change to this Step's original label-driven design, flagged for validation | §8.2, §5.2, §21 |
| 48 | sentinels + suggestions | Coverage & doc-drift sentinels, apply-suggestion via validated endpoint; rebuttals identified by the finding's own persisted content (hash/text stored at post time, Step 45), never by file:line alone, feeding re-review reconciliation as deterministic "already answered" facts prepended to (not replacing) the prose fallback; sentinel auto-fix — child session opens a stacked follow-up PR, cherry-picked onto main and merge-gated once the origin PR merges (§17.4's existing four merge-time checks, unchanged in count); the origin+fix pair is additionally registered as a real GitHub stack via `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/stacks` once both PRs exist — a second call after creation, never a substitute for it, logged and ignored on a 404/any other failure rather than failing the flow (**amendment**, §17.2/§17.6); the fix PR's base is the origin PR's own head branch by construction and must never route through the happy-path default-branch resolution (`resolvePRBaseBranch`, `pushpr.go`) that Step 21's ordinary PR-creation flow uses (**amendment**, §17.2); the child session's write/edit tool capability is additionally restricted server-side to test/doc path patterns at spawn time (**new**, §17.2) — a second, independent layer alongside §17.4's post-hoc diff-scope check, not a fifth check within it; admin-only toggle off by default | §8.2, §17, §17.6 |
| 49 | handoff-readiness sentinel | Dedicated review sentinel on PRs from scoped sessions (Step 10): uncontracted endpoints (Step 27), backend TODOs left by the agent; **v1** = `handoff` label + summary posted as a comment (+ optional linked issue); **v2 deferred** (child session + plan mode) only if handoff volume justifies it | §14.4, §14.5 |
| 50 | release PR review | Release detection (`release/*` branch/label, via classifier §8.3); **manifest** (always): list PRs merged since the last release point, check review + CI-at-merge-SHA + reverts for each, posted via the server-side verdict tool; **conditional aggregate diff** (pure rule: ≥3 PRs in the same subsystem, a critical-tier PR present, or a manually-resolved conflict), dedicated composition/interaction prompt, never a PR-by-PR re-review | §15 |
| 51 | automations: engine | Automation→invocation→runs, CAS failure strikes, auto-pause at 3, recovery sweeps | §3.5 |
| 52 | automations: triggers & extras | GitHub/Linear/webhook/cron conditions, sandboxSettings, `artifact_summary`, per-automation secrets | §8.4 |
| 53 | provider credential injection | Generic secret injection into the OpenCode process's own `cmd.Env` (`internal/sandboxagent/opencodeproc/spawn.go`), closing a pre-existing gap that blocks per-turn/per-step model override for EVERY provider (Anthropic, OpenAI, Gemini alike) — not a Gemini-specific cost. Verified directly: `spawn.go` starts `opencode serve` inheriting sandbox-agent's own OS environment verbatim (`Env: supervisor.EnvWithout(boot.SessionConfigEnvVar)`, exactly one variable excluded); no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY`/Google-equivalent is wired anywhere, even though the model-selection passthrough (`turns.model_id` → `buildPromptPayload` → `sandboxws.Prompt.model` → the adapter's `strings.Cut(raw, "/")`, no Narvi-side allowlist) already works end-to-end. `ActionManageRepoSecrets`/`ActionManageEnvSecrets`/`ActionManageGlobalSecrets` (`authorize.go`) already reserve the RBAC row for this class of data, but no secret-storage table exists anywhere in this codebase yet (grepped directly) — this Step is the first one that actually builds it, scoped to provider API keys, mapped provider→env-var name. Blocking prerequisite for Steps 54-56 | §25.1, §25.3 |
| 54 | domain/workflow + loopguard + schema | `internal/domain/workflow` (pure, no I/O): `Lane` enum (review/request/plan) atop the classifier's existing `Target`/`Mode` vocabulary (`intent.TargetReview`/`TargetRequest`, `intent.ModePlan`/`ModeBuild`, `rubric.go`); `WorkflowDefinition`/`StepDefinition`/`Edge`/`StepOutcomeStatus` (closed 3-value enum: ok/needs_fix/blocked — a distinct axis from `Shippable`, never routed through it); `NextStep`, one pure decision function, same shape as `turn.Transition`/`plan.Transition`/`sandbox.Transition`. `internal/domain/loopguard`: generic circuit breaker, `Evaluate(State, Config) Decision`, no time window — iteration count reads `COUNT(*)` on `workflow_step_runs`, no dedicated counter column. Migrations: `workflow_definitions` (3 seeded `is_built_in = true` rows), `workflow_step_definitions`, `workflow_edges`, `workflow_bindings`, `workflow_runs`, `workflow_step_runs`; `/contracts` additions; the 3 new RBAC actions. Dark — no dispatch wiring yet, no behavior change | §25.4, §25.5, §25.10, §25.11 |
| 55 | workflow execution engine | `internal/app/workflowengine`, wired into `createTurnLocked`/`CreateTurnCore` and `sessionactor`'s `handleSandboxEvent` — exercised by 100% of production traffic from day one: the three built-in workflows (review, request passthrough, plan's existing 2-step approve/build) become how every session actually dispatches, not an opt-in path alongside the old one. Zero-config proof: the built-in review/request workflows have a single step whose `PromptTemplate` is a passthrough render of the caller's own text and `ModelID = nil` (inherits `turns.model_id`/`sessions.build_model_id` exactly as today). Exit criterion: a characterization test runs the old direct path and the new engine path on the same inputs and diffs the resulting `sandboxws.Prompt` JSON for strict equality | §25.6, §25.7, §25.8 |
| 56 | workflow HITL gate + circuit breaker | Approval state machine plus three verdicts (approve/reject/revise — `revise` is always a re-execution of the same step with the human's text folded in as an extra instruction, never a direct substitution of a structured artifact, mirroring plan mode's own `revise:` handling today); `NotificationKind` extensions to the existing Slack (`planslacknotifier.go`)/Linear (`linearnotifier.go`) notifiers, following the same pattern already used twice; `loopguard.Evaluate` consulted only when the `audit → fix` edge is about to re-fire, wired to the `needs_fix` edge; `POST /api/workflow-runs/:runId/steps/:stepRunId/decide`, same shape as `decideplan.go`. Human-revision loops are exempt from the circuit breaker, mirroring §24.6's own manual-retrigger exemption | §25.9 |
| 57 ∥ | RWX provider + previews | RWX adapter (pinned-CLI transport, real-binary contract tests, capabilities from verified support — `Resume` settled empirically as the first exit criterion); preview-app dispatch + commit-status preview links at the latest PR commit via two new outbox kinds, enqueued on `push_complete` alongside the first real `preview`-typed artifact writer; per-repo setting, off by default | §4.1.1, §4.1.2, §8.9 |
| 58 ∥ | uploads | Object storage (S3-compatible) + `download_file` tool, failed-upload signal | §8.6, §28 |
| 59 | models | Catalog, Codex via ChatGPT-account OAuth, per-session/per-message reasoning effort; shadow-comparison tooling for review; **Codex/ChatGPT OAuth finding RESOLVED** (full design §29, verified 2026-08-06 live against the same pinned binary: ChatGPT Pro/Plus OAuth is NATIVE in OpenCode 1.17.15 — `GET /provider/auth` lists browser + headless (device-code) oauth methods for `openai`, no plugin and no new `AgentRuntime` work — but it is NOT env-var-shaped: the credential is injected via OpenCode's own `PUT /auth/openai` after spawn, stored per-USER via a `provider_credentials` `user`-scope + `oauth`-kind extension, linked once from Settings via the device flow (`auth.openai.com/codex/device` — a Narvi-hosted web callback is impossible, the client id's redirect is pinned to `localhost:1455`), and refreshed centrally by a single CP pump because OpenAI rotates refresh tokens with reuse detection); **reasoning-effort finding RESOLVED** (§29.8: `turns.effort` + `sessions.build_effort` nullable columns mirroring `model_id`/`build_model_id`'s own threading verbatim → the already-present required-nullable `sandboxws.Prompt.effort` → `prompt_async`'s own verified `variant` field, valid values owned per-model by OpenCode's catalog `variants` maps, no Narvi-side enum); **Gemini finding RESOLVED** (verified 2026-08-03 against the live pinned OpenCode 1.17.15 binary's own `GET /provider` catalog, bypassing rtk's output-truncation — lists a `google` provider (env `GOOGLE_API_KEY`/`GOOGLE_GENERATIVE_AI_API_KEY`/`GEMINI_API_KEY`) plus `google-vertex`/`google-vertex-anthropic`, with real models e.g. `gemini-3.5-flash` carrying `capabilities.toolcall: true`, a 1M-token context window and real cost data — so no new `AgentRuntime` adapter is needed for Gemini, only Step 53's credential injection plus a CI contract test, mirroring the existing pinned-binary contract-test discipline, verifying Gemini's actual tool-calling/streaming quality through OpenCode, since every existing contract test is Claude-backed and proves nothing transitively about a different provider's behavior) | §8.8, §25.2, §29 |
| 60 | decision inbox: read model + API | Read-only aggregation (plans, review sessions, sessions, automations, outbox) + `ListOpenPRsForUser` / `ResolveCodeOwners` on the SourceControl port (CODEOWNERS→persons via the identity graph §13.2, short-TTL cache with displayed staleness); ready_to_merge / needs_review / awaiting_approval / needs_attention taxonomy; per-item assignment provenance; Merge endpoint with **server-side re-validation at click time** (CI + approval + Authorize); decision-latency metric | §16 |
| 61 | domain/turn: builder epistemic pre-action check | Global env-default + per-session override flag (mirrors `plan_mode`'s own nullable-column threading); a devil's-advocate preamble injected into non-planning build turns only (folded into the existing plan-mode risks section for planning turns, no duplicate mechanism); the agent additionally emits a structured, typed field (`EpistemicOutcome`: `none/minor/strong`, persisted on the turn row) alongside its natural-language reply — never prompt-only; analytics-only signal, off by default, no hard gate until telemetry justifies one | §20 |
| 62 | review verdict persistence, analytics, digest & automated approval | Append-only `review_verdicts` history (one row per posted verdict, Step 45's structured type — no parsing, since it's already typed), `DISTINCT ON` latest-per-PR reduction, bounded by an explicit active/recent window from day one; **auto-approval eligibility engine**: server-recomputed `Shippable` plus deterministic criteria (CI green, no floor raised, diff size under a configurable threshold, no sensitive path touched — migrations/auth/contracts, configurable per repo), no per-PR human label required, the `review: needs-human` label (Step 47) excludes a PR regardless of criteria; **decoupled per-repo auto-merge toggle, off by default**: while off, auto-approved PRs surface in the decision inbox (§16/Step 60) as "ready to merge (auto)" with 1-click confirm; contradiction-rate calibration read model (auto-approved-then-human-contested) accumulates from day one for the toggle decision; auto-merge, once armed, reuses the decision inbox's server-side re-validation-at-click contract (§16/Step 60) unchanged, just machine-initiated; analytics rollup/timeseries/top-risk endpoints with an explicit "not yet computed" sentinel distinct from a real zero; deterministic (non-LLM), per-repo/per-channel daily digest reusing the decision inbox's assignment logic (§16) for scoping, claim-before-act per (date, channel) via `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` | §21 |
| 63 | review: learned false-positive patterns | Repo-scoped table of maintainer-taught false-positive descriptions, captured via a `false positive: <reason>` command (maintainer+ direct via the existing `Authorize` gate, Step 39 — immediate effect, no propose/validate flow), upserted idempotently keyed on the triggering comment id; injected into every review (first pass and re-review alike) as an advisory, explicitly-untrusted content block ("weigh it, verify independently, do not skip a legitimate finding"); retire, hit-count, and an audit view ship in this same Step — no deferred lifecycle; **rebuttal identity is content-based, never file:line** — `reviewpost.Finding` carries a server-computed `StartLine`/`EndLine` from a sliding-window match of the finding's own stored snippet against the diff, falling back to one small non-agentic call on the existing `LLM` port (§4.3) when the match fails, and to an explicit, un-guessed `0` (never a stale line number) when both fail; ships as original Step scope, no migration | §22 |
| 64 | plan mode: follow-up intent classification (amend vs answer) | New `plan_followup` surface on the existing unified intent classifier (§18), single call site gated on "a plan exists and is `awaiting_approval`"; persisted `answer_only` flag on the turn/message row, consulted at the turn-creation chokepoint (`httpapi.createTurnLocked`) before any dispatch decision (never trusting the sandbox/runtime to self-enforce, and never the plan-save path, which runs too late to gate dispatch since a turn is already created and dispatched by the time any plan-save logic runs); on classifier failure or low confidence, fail open to **wait-for-clarification** — nothing dispatched, the plan stays `awaiting_approval`, an honest reply asks the user to approve/reject/clarify, never a silent build-turn dispatch (the interim deterministic gate's own hold-and-clarify behavior, which remains the floor under any failure mode); replaces the interim gate's `revise:`-prefix requirement with real amend-vs-answer classification — the prefix stays as a deterministic override bypassing classification; classifier surface excluded from any public route by construction (private to `app/`, never registered on `httpapi`/`wshub`) | §23 |
| 65 | review: automatic re-review on new commits | New GitHub `pull_request`/`synchronize` ingress lane, reusing the webhook toolkit's claim/release (Step 31) for delivery dedupe and `github_pr_sessions` (Step 32) for per-PR routing to the existing review session, never a new mapping table; trailing-edge debounce via a new named `session_timers` entry (§2 — re-arming on every push is the SAME upsert idiom the 5 existing named timers already use, so a burst reviews once, at the burst's OWN final head, after a quiet period); trigger state read from `review_verdicts.head_sha` (Step 62) compared against the PR's current head, never a bot-written label; off by default, per-repository opt-in, fails closed if the setting can't be read; never auto-approves anything on its own; a per-PR re-review budget breaks an automated-fix-triggers-automated-review loop, falling back to the existing manual label/button re-trigger once exhausted | §24 |
| 66 | review digest: verdict as merge readout | Rendered verdict restructured to front-load the merge decision — "What this PR does" (2-4 sentences written from the diff, never copied from the description; the §26.2 adequacy reference and the §16 inbox headline), "Architecture choices" (decision, implicitly-rejected alternative, convention conformance), "Risks to the stack" (`BlastRadius` vocabulary + coupling/deploy risks + reversibility + explicit not-verified limits), findings/coverage/docs-drift demoted to a collapsed appendix; all typed fields on the Step 47 posting payload (`Digest{Summary, ArchDecisions[], StackRisks, UnverifiedLimits}`), never markers re-parsed from markdown (Step 45's invariant); `Summary` required on every review from day one, full digest schema-required on the deep path once Step 68 defines it (reject-don't-repair at the posting endpoint); digest columns ride `review_verdicts` (Step 62) so digest quality is measurable from day one | §26.1 |
| 67 | review digest: description adequacy + graduated remediation | Typed `DescriptionAdequacy: ok/drift/misleading` + one-line explanation, judged by comparing the diff-derived `Digest.Summary` against title+body (which stay untrusted input, §5.2); a THIRD raise-only floor — `misleading` → `Shippable ≥ needs_human`, composing via the existing `max(rank)` (Step 45's one-pure-function-per-floor pattern), deliberately never inflating `RiskLevel`; remediation graduated by authorship: Narvi-authored PRs may get their **body** rewritten behind a per-repo `descriptionAutofix` flag (default off), original preserved in a collapsed block, delivered via a `SourceControl` port extension + outbox (§5.1) with authorship+flag checks server-side (§5.2); human PRs get a proposed body in the digest only; the title is never rewritten automatically | §26.2 |
| 68 | review triage: deterministic light/deep routing | Depth decided in the single review funnel (Step 46's creation/dispatch path), deterministic-first (§18 posture): diff stats (already fetched), changed paths promoted to a structured signal, sensitive globs (migrations/auth/infra-as-code/CI) mapped onto `BlastRadius` tags, top-level-root dispersion, provenance (Narvi vs human + authoring model), Step 62 verdict history, the `review:needs-human` label (human-owned, never bot-synced — unlike `review:low/medium/high-risk`, which `ComputeLabelSync` overwrites on every verdict and so is deliberately not a signal here); v1 rules: sensitive glob → deep, >600 lines or ≥3 roots → deep, a prior `high` verdict → deep, `review:needs-human` → deep, else light — no LLM tie-break (v2 option only if per-path analytics show a grey zone); output `reviewDepth: light/deep` threaded into session creation, recorded on the §18.4 routing decision record, persisted as `review_path` on the verdict row so cost AND precision are measurable per path; depth drives review model/effort (light = balanced tier, deep = frontier + high effort); per-repo `reviewDepth: {mode, deepPaths}`; ANY triage error fails open to light; §24 re-review re-evaluates on the delta but floors at the PR's previous depth; the light/deep fork itself is independently validated by outside production precedent for gating whole passes on a size signal — cited in full at §26.3; v1 ships no further *within*-deep-path pass gating (a PR already routed deep keeps every deep-path pass regardless of its own size), named and deferred to a telemetry-justified v2 (§26.9) **Amended (2026-09-28):** an unreadable input is not a router error: Step 199 records it with its own reason and routes it deep, and the fail-open-to-light rule stays for the router's own errors | §26.3 |
| 69 | review deep path: adversarial counter-review + readout measurement | One sandbox, sub-task fan-out (§7.1 — never N sandboxes, never §14.4 child sessions): read-only `architecture-scribe` (virgin-context arch recap) + read-only adversarial `counter-reviewer` (refutes findings, hunts misses; pinnable to an opposing model family via Step 53's credentials + Step 59's catalog); only findings surviving counter-review are published, inter-agent disagreements rendered as a "Contested points" digest section; typed `CounterReview: done/skipped` schema-required on the deep path, `skipped` floors `Shippable` to `needs_human`; measurement on Step 62's instrument — per-section digest feedback kinds + a maintainer `arch recap wrong: <reason>` command (mirrors Step 63's command: `Authorize`-gated, deterministically routable §5.2, idempotent on comment id); KPIs: digest contestation rate, decision latency per path, cost per path, % PRs approved with zero human inline comments; digest-quality evals join the shadow-precision discipline; **a diff-only fact-check pass runs on BOTH paths**, one more sub-task the primary orchestrates (§7.1) with no tool access, that kills a finding only when it is provably wrong from the diff text alone and lets anything merely uncertain through untouched — on the deep path it runs before counter-review, pruning what the adversarial pass has to adjudicate; typed `FactCheck: done/skipped` + `FactCheckKilled int`, schema-required on both paths, `skipped` never floors `Shippable` (it can only under-filter, never miss a real defect) and is itself non-fatal on error (fails open, same posture as triage); **a per-path cost budget** checks accumulated spend (§7.1's existing cost roll-up) against a per-repo-tunable ceiling before dispatching each optional sub-task — never a prediction of that sub-task's own cost — and a breach simply reports as that sub-task's own existing `skipped` state, no new outcome field | §26.4, §26.5, §26.6, §26.7 |
| 70 | review: wire the cost budget | Step 69 shipped `ShouldSkipOptionalPass` as a tested, exported pure function with zero production callers — its own doc comment says so; this Step gives it one. Builds the per-turn `step_finish.cost` accumulator §26.7's mechanism assumes (§7.1's own text previously claimed this rollup already existed; it did not — corrected as part of this Step) as a new `turnState.spentUSD` field on the OpenCode adapter (`internal/adapters/outbound/opencode`), summed in `dispatchPart`'s `"step-finish"` case for every step-finish the turn observes, main lane and every sub-task alike. **As actually shipped, the accumulator and its one production call site both live entirely inside the sandbox, never crossing back to the control plane**: `cmd/sandbox-agent` gained its first HTTP server, a loopback-only (`127.0.0.1`, ephemeral port, no authentication needed) listener answering `GET /review-cost-budget?ceilingUsd=<usd>` by reading the turn's own live `spentUSD` and calling `ShouldSkipOptionalPass` for real. The review turn's own prompt (`internal/domain/review`'s `subAgentOrchestrationInstructions`) now instructs the agent to `GET` this endpoint before spawning `counter-reviewer`/`fact-check` and obey its `shouldSkip` answer — replacing the prior self-estimation instruction — with the SAME placeholder-substitution mechanism the verdict-posting tool's URL already uses (a new `{{REVIEW_COST_BUDGET_TOOL_URL}}` token, resolved by sandbox-agent immediately before the prompt reaches the engine) as the one place the mechanism's own design does originate control-plane-side (at turn-creation/prompt-render time) before being consumed sandbox-side. On any failure calling the endpoint (tool-use error, timeout, non-2xx, malformed body), the prompt instructs the agent to treat that identically to `shouldSkip: true` — fail-safe toward skip, never toward proceeding. Implements §26.9's decided exclusion of `architecture-scribe` from budget gating (only `counter-reviewer` and the fact-check pass have a typed `skipped` outcome the budget can trip; gating the scribe too would floor nothing and be recorded nowhere) — the prompt text never even names the scribe in the budget-check instructions on the light path, and explicitly carves it out by name on the deep path | §26.5, §26.7, §26.9, §7.1 |
| 71 | review: post-hoc sub-task corroboration | Closes §26.4's own named residual: a schema-required `CounterReview: done` is presence-verified by the schema but truth-verified by nothing — a reviewer that never dispatched the sub-task can still report `done`. `sub_task_finish` is one of the six ack-guaranteed critical event types and every recognized sandbox event is persisted unconditionally, so the trace to corroborate against already exists; this Step is what reads it back and compares. What §26.4's heading calls "structural enforcement" only becomes honestly so once this ships | §26.4, §7.1 |

**Phase 5 milestone**: code review exercised end-to-end on live PRs of repositories where posting is acceptable (Narvi's own), every verdict reviewed for precision before being trusted. An earlier wording — "code review in shadow on live PRs" — promised a capability this plan did not yet contain: log-only operation against a repository Narvi must leave no trace in requires Phase 8's egress-suppression machinery (Steps 96-104, §30), not just §9.4's precision-review discipline. Shadow evaluation on customer repositories is Phase 8's milestone, not this one's.

## Phase 6 — Rollout (7 Steps)

**Placement rule this plan applies whenever a Step is scoped:** review/automation work belongs in
Phase 5 regardless of when in the project it's designed, even after Phase 5's own milestone line —
see Steps 70-71 above. Rollout-enabling platform glue belongs here in Phase 6 instead. Steps 72-74
below are the clearest case: they deliver the §8 item 5 feature set ("Enterprise sandbox glue") —
`docs/TECHNICAL_PLAN.md` §27 — and open Phase 6 rather than landing in Phase 5, because §10-P6's own
first line ("Config setup: automations, secrets, environments, settings, integrations") already
presumes these surfaces exist, and Step 75 (config/data seeding), which opens the rest of this
phase, seeds exactly what they build.

| Step | Title | Content | Ref. |
|---|---|---|---|
| 72 | sandbox secrets & opencode config | General `sandbox_secrets` table (scope ENUM `automation`/`environment`/`repo`/`global` — the `automation` slot is schema-only, its CRUD/consumption stays with the per-automation follow-up Step 52 deferred; resolution automation → environment → repo → global via the SAME generic `providercredential.Resolve`, one new `scopePriority` row), Step 53's idioms reused throughout (partial-unique-index pair, `platform.EncryptToken` at rest, the three already-reserved `ActionManage*Secrets` RBAC actions, write-only management API); name validation fail-closed (POSIX shape; `NARVI_*`, the whole `OPENCODE_*` namespace and `providercredential.EnvVarNames` names rejected — one owning mechanism per env-var name), enforced BOTH at save time and again by sandbox-agent over the delivered map; delivery via `POST /sessions/{id}/sandbox-secrets` mirroring `providercredentialsdelivery.go`'s handshake verbatim (bearer, dead-sandbox 410, gen 403, decrypt-only-winners); sandbox-agent fetches before the first hook, with bounded retry, and threads the map into every spawned process (hooks via `hooks.go:141`'s `EnvWithout` seam, services, `opencode serve`), warn-and-continue on fetch failure, NEVER passed to `BuildImage` (§19.8 rule (a)). OpenCode config storage (`opencode_configs`, plaintext JSONB — secrets belong in `sandbox_secrets` via OpenCode's own `{env:VAR}` substitution) + injection into OpenCode's OWN documented config slots: global doc → `~/.config/opencode/opencode.json`, environment doc → `OPENCODE_CONFIG` env var, so the engine's own precedence keeps repo project config and the Step 48 sentinel-fix restriction above every Narvi-injected document | §27.1, §27.2 |
| 73 | cloud identity: OIDC federation + kubeconfig | CP becomes an OIDC issuer (`/.well-known/openid-configuration` + JWKS on a new validated `platform.Config` public issuer URL — capability off and binding CRUD refusing, fail-closed, when unset); RS256 keys encrypted at rest via `platform.EncryptToken`, rotation = overlapping JWKS publication (§5.2's grace-window discipline); `sub` stable and per-Environment (`narvi:environment:<id>` — Azure's exact-match constraint; session-varying context only in custom claims), `aud` per customer binding, `exp` ≈ 10 min; `cloud_identity_bindings` (environment/global scope, kind aws/gcp/azure/generic, params are identifiers not secrets, deliberately no repo scope) + minting endpoint `POST /sessions/{id}/cloud-identity-token` (same sandbox-bearer handshake; refuses any audience no binding declares); sandbox-agent maintains per-binding token files under `/narvi/identity/` (0700/0600, never a repo tree), half-life refresh, and sets each cloud's STANDARD file-sourced env vars (`AWS_WEB_IDENTITY_TOKEN_FILE`+`AWS_ROLE_ARN`, `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`→external-account JSON, `AZURE_FEDERATED_TOKEN_FILE`+ids) — the clouds' own SDKs do the STS exchange, zero per-cloud Narvi code. Kubeconfig: per-Environment `cluster_bindings` (UNIQUE, v1 = one cluster), rendered at boot with `KUBECONFIG` set; auth rungs cloud (exec plugins riding the same env vars) > oidc (kube-apiserver trusts Narvi's issuer; authenticates via the kubeconfig's own `tokenFile` field, pointed at a token minted/refreshed through the SAME §27.3 mint-and-refresh mechanism as a cloud_identity_bindings token — **corrected mid-Step by adversarial review**: the original design used a exec plugin, a new sandbox-agent `kube-credential` subcommand printing `ExecCredential` JSON on the `runCredentialHelper` subcommand precedent, which shipped structurally non-functional — `kube-credential` needed `NARVI_SESSION_CONFIG` to mint anything, but unlike `git` (which sandbox-agent spawns itself, inheriting its own env), `kubectl` is only ever run by the agent inside the already-`NARVI_SESSION_CONFIG`-stripped `opencode serve`/hook/services.yml tree, so the subcommand had no environment to read that variable from and failed on every invocation; the `tokenFile` fix needs no subcommand, no re-exec, and no new IPC surface) > static (uploaded kubeconfig stored through Step 72's secrets, named as the discouraged lowest rung) | §27.3, §27.4 |
| 74 ∥ | sandbox substrate: docker, egress policy, toolchain | Per-Environment `docker: required` flag carried in SESSION_CONFIG AND as a top-level `CreateSpec` field (`createspec.go`'s Gen-style deliberate-duplicate-with-`Validate` discipline); `ports.Capabilities` gains `DockerInSandbox`/`EgressPolicy`; fail-closed twice (session creation refused up-front on an unsupporting provider, re-checked at dispatch — never silently unenforced); Modal adapter maps the flag onto its VM-runtime option (default gVisor sandboxes can't run dockerd cleanly; privileged mode rejected outright, §5.2); dockerd supervised as one more entry in the existing supervision table with a named `boot_progress` phase; enforced per-Environment `egress_policy {open|allowlist}` at the provider substrate with a server-appended allowlist floor (CP host + git hosts); cooperative `HTTP(S)_PROXY`/`NO_PROXY` routing is pure Step 72 env plumbing, named to prevent a parallel mechanism; toolchain baked into the BASE image (Playwright+Chromium pinned with OS deps, ripgrep, typescript-language-server, docker CLI/engine dormant-by-default, the three cloud exec-credential plugins), versions visible via the existing boot-fingerprint image digest; new §9.3-class restore-with-docker scenario before snapshot support is claimed | §27.5, §27.6, §27.7 |
| 75 | config/data seeding | Scripts to seed automations, secrets, environments, settings, integrations; participants→users mapping (by GitHub id, default member) | §10-P6, §13.4 |
| 76 | cohort rollout | Feature-flagged cohort rollout of sessions, documented rollback | §10-P6, §32 |
| 77 | ops | Dashboards/alerts (false failures, outbox lag, orphans, boot p95), runbooks from the resilience catalog (§9.3) | §5.3 |
| 78 | launch readiness | Production checklist, SLO alerts wired, on-call runbook; a per-surface user guide (web/Slack/Linear/GitHub) documenting what each surface accepts AND its honest negatives, shipped behavior only — a CI check ties every documented command to the `/contracts` route or classifier routing record (§18.4) that actually implements it, so the guide can never drift into aspirational text | §10-P6 |

**Phase 6 milestone**: the platform serves production traffic under monitoring — a cohort flag
moves real sessions with the documented rollback exercised at least once, Step 77's dashboards
and alerts fire on real signals rather than synthetic ones, and `docs/PRODUCTION_CHECKLIST.md`
is green. The monitoring half of that sentence is what Step 111 (Phase 10) makes real: until an
OTLP backend can evaluate an alert, this milestone rests on instruments nobody aggregates, and
Phase 10's own header says exactly that.

## Phase 7 — UI, and the backend surfaces it needs (17 Steps, visual spec = nine-view mockups)

Fourteen UI Steps and two backend ones. The two (88, 90) are here rather than in an earlier phase
because nothing before Phase 7 needed them, and rather than in Phase 11 because they **block**
scheduled work — 88 is what 91 and 93 call, 90 is what 92 calls. Phase 11 is explicitly for gaps
that block nothing, and a prerequisite filed there would both violate that rule and sit at a higher
Step number than its own consumer, breaking the property that a Step's number states its position.

They exist because three of the four UI rows after 87 turned out to assume a REST surface that was
never built: the DTOs, the RBAC actions and the engines all shipped, and no handler reached any of
them. Checking a row against the surface it assumes, before starting it, is cheap and was not being
done.

| Step | Title | Content | Ref. |

| 88 | backend: workflow definition & run API | §25.10's route table, none of which exists: the DTOs, the three RBAC actions and the engine all shipped, but no HTTP handler reaches any of them, so the canvas and the run view have nothing to call. Definition CRUD as one whole document (steps and edges embedded, complete desired state on PUT, re-validated server-side against the closed model so a graph the engine cannot execute is refused at save, never at draw); binding list + admin-only activate; read-only run and step-run reads, a run always returned with its ordered step runs. **Closes §25.11's own broken premise:** the maintainer-level row is justified by editing an *unbound draft*, but `LoadDefinition` reads by id alone and never consults `definition_version`, so editing a bound definition changes production dispatch immediately, reaching past the admin-only activation gate. PUT/DELETE on a bound definition is therefore refused structurally, exactly as `is_built_in` is — duplicate, edit the copy, an admin activates | §25.10, §25.11, §25.4 |
| 89 | ui repository settings | The per-repository surface has no screen of its own: `repo_settings` carries 15 configurable columns and eight RBAC actions that were previously homeless, folded into Step 86's row alongside Environments, members, audit and analytics. Its own view: the four toggles (`auto_merge_enabled`, `auto_retrigger_review_enabled`, `sentinel_autofix_enabled`, `description_autofix_enabled`), `block_on_high_risk` + `max_auto_approve_files_changed` (§21's auto-approval floor), review-depth mode + deep paths (§26.8), the light/deep cost budgets (§26.7), `sensitive_blast_radius_tags`, and the three `rwx_preview_*` fields (§4.1.2). Repo identity resolved server-side through the existing known-repo gate, never a caller-supplied name. `sessions_enabled` is deliberately ABSENT: §32.6 makes enrollment seed-manifest-only, and a UI toggle would reintroduce the chicken-and-egg that rule exists to avoid — the screen says so rather than hiding it | §21, §26.7, §26.8, §4.1.2 |
| 90 | backend: integrations read model & preview config | Two small surfaces the screens after it need and cannot reach. §12.5's `GET /api/integrations`: one row per ingress surface (Slack/Linear/GitHub) saying whether it is configured and carrying its last-delivery evidence — derived from `platform.Config` plus existing `webhook_deliveries`/outbox rows, no new table, no connect/disconnect write, never the secrets themselves in any form, and a timestamped fact rather than a health verdict. Plus §4.1.2's three `rwx_preview_*` columns, which exist in Postgres and are read only by `previewpr.go`: `endpointTemplate`/`orgSlug` read and write normally, `dispatchKey` write-only with a fixed non-secret placeholder, the same rule every other credential in this codebase follows — the gap Step 89 shipped an honest "not configurable here" notice for | §12.5, §4.1.2, §13.3 |
| 91 | workflow canvas editor | React Flow-style node/edge canvas for authoring a workflow's steps and edges per lane/repo; validates/constrains what a user can draw against the engine's closed model (ordered steps + 3-status edges, no expression language, §25.4) — a drawn graph the engine cannot execute must be rejected at save time, not silently accepted. Inline progress display of a running workflow in the session view is a SMALL extension of the already-planned sub-task-lane rendering (§7.1, Steps 82/83), not a separate Step | §25.12, §7.1 |
| 92 | ui integrations & provider accounts | Every surface connecting this platform to something outside it, on one screen because they share a shape (connect → verify → show liveness → disconnect) and nothing else claims them: integrations management for the Slack/Linear/GitHub ingress surfaces (`ActionManageIntegrations`, admin) with per-surface connection state and last-delivery evidence; ChatGPT-account (Codex) linking over the device flow already shipped (§29.3) — start/poll/confirm, the link's expiry, and the refresh pump's health, since a silently-expired link degrades model availability with no other signal; and cloud-identity signing-key rotation (§27.3, admin) showing the JWKS overlap window explicitly, because rotating the trust anchor customer clouds federate to is exactly the operation whose in-flight state an operator must see. Fails closed like every other §27.3 surface: no rotation affordance at all when the capability is unconfigured | §29.3, §27.3, §13.3 |
| 93 | backend: per-step model & cost attribution | The run view the next Step builds is specified to show **per-step model and cost**, and neither reaches it today. Cost is not persisted anywhere: the only accumulator is `internal/adapters/outbound/opencode`'s own in-process `turnState.spentUSD`, which answers one sandbox-local question (§26.7's budget check) and dies with the turn — §7.1 records this as debt in its own words, "left to whichever future work actually adds it", and §25.13 says this chantier "inherits, and must close" it at the workflow-step level. This is that work. Model needs no wire change and no new column: `turns.model_id` already persists what was dispatched, and a workflow step IS an ordinary turn (§25.6), so it is a join. Cost does: `step_finish.cost` (§6.1) already arrives per step but is only ever stored inside an `events` payload, and `events` has no `turn_id`, so a running total is accumulated onto the turn as those events land. The "edge actually taken" is deliberately NOT a new field — it is derivable from the ordered step runs and their outcome statuses, and inventing a column for a fact already on the wire would be the third copy of a truth. Out of scope, named not built: cross-session cost totals, and §12.2 item 6's cost-by-model view | §25.15, §6.1, §7.1 |
| 94 | ui workflow runs & decisions | Step 91 authors a workflow; this one watches it run and unblocks it. A run view over the existing `workflow_runs`/`workflow_step_runs` read model — step sequence, current status, per-step model and cost, and the edge actually taken — plus the human decision gate the engine already persists: approve, reject, and revise, where **revise always re-runs the step with the human text as an additional instruction** and never substitutes a structured artifact, matching the semantics those same tables encode for plan mode. Without this the engine can pause a run awaiting a human and no surface can answer it. Decisions are own/joined-aware (`ActionDecideWorkflowStep`); an exhausted circuit breaker is shown as a terminal reason and never a retry button, since §24.6's budget exemption covers manual re-trigger, not a spent breaker | §7.1, §13.3 |
| 95 | ui finalize | `make dist` single self-contained binary, screenshot review vs mockups, ship | §12.4 |

**Phase 7 milestone**: all nine views of §12.2 built to the mockups, including the
decision-inbox home (§16), plus the UX items of §12.3; a screenshot-level review against
`docs/design/mockups.html`; `make dist` producing the single self-contained binary. Where a
view honestly renders "not available yet" because the data behind it does not exist, that
state is part of what the review signs off rather than a defect it hides — those rows are
Phase 11's, named there one at a time.

## Phase 8 — Platform shadow mode (9 Steps)

Zero-trace evaluation on live customer repositories — every customer-visible egress surface
suppressed and recorded, the sandbox starved of write capability, and "Activate" as the graduation
gesture (technical plan §30). On the Phase 5/6 placement rule above: by its letter this is
rollout-enabling platform glue, but it is not folded into Phase 6 — it is a platform-wide chantier
with its own technical-plan section, cutting across the outbound adapters, the outbox, every
ingress handler, sandbox-agent, and Settings, and Phase 6's own Steps were long since scoped.
Inserting it mid-sequence would shift Steps 75-95 (the Phase 4 standing rule) and buy nothing:
no Phase 6/7 Step depends on it. It therefore lands appended, with execution order stated
explicitly instead of implied by numbering — §10-P8: this phase is the bridge between Phase 5's
milestone and any customer-facing activation, schedulable regardless of Phase 6/7 status (only
Step 104's UI surface leans on Phase 7, via Step 86's Settings screens). Three §30.9 decisions gate
individual Step shapes (chat triggers → Step 102; git mirror → Step 103; ledger visibility →
Step 104) and are resolved at those Steps, never silently defaulted.

| Step | Title | Content | Ref. |
|---|---|---|---|
| 96 | shadow flag & resolver | `repo_settings.live_egress_enabled bool NOT NULL DEFAULT false` migration (polarity chosen so the established fail-closed settings-read idiom — `reviewretrigger.go`, `appreviewverdict.AutoMergeEnabled` — yields suppress-on-error for free, and every newly connected repo starts in shadow); `NARVI_SHADOW_MODE` master switch (per-process; documented "dedicated evaluation deployments only, never flipped on a running fleet" — a rolling restart makes a mixed fleet where a still-live pod delivers rows a shadow pod enqueued); resolver package exposing the egress capability token (the only source of a live/pass-through gate, unexported constructor) and the resolution function with a pinned test: suppress on `ErrNoRows`, on arbitrary error, on absent row; flag flips journaled to `audit_log`. Dark — the mode exists and resolves, nothing consumes it yet | §30.8 |
| 97 | github egress gate + suppression ledger | Deny-by-default shadow `http.RoundTripper` inside `githubapi` (GET/HEAD pass; every mutating verb intercepted, recorded, and answered with a synthesized transport-level success as a fallback net — the only layer containing a future 12th mutating method on the concrete type and the ingress handlers' synchronous comments; for the 6 `ports.SourceControl` writes below, the port decorator's own typed result is what a caller actually observes, this transport fallback is the net beneath it, never the primary path); the four outbound constructors (`githubapi`, `slackapi`, `linearapi`, `rwx`) lose their `nil → http.DefaultClient` defaults — gate argument mandatory and non-nil, zero value fail-closed; explicit non-embedded `ports.SourceControl` decorator (a new port method breaks the build until handled); `shadow_scm_writes` migration + record-or-fail semantics (suppression returns success only if the ledger insert commits); record types with no `Token` field by construction; for 5 of the 6 decorated writes, a synthetic result built only through a dedicated unexported constructor (negative numbers + `shadow://` scheme, never collidable with a real GitHub URL — `isPlatformAuthored` is a string match and PR numbers are monotonic), pinned by a test that the constructor is the only production call site; the sixth, `MergePR`, returns no synthetic result at all — a typed `ShadowSuppressed` sentinel instead, mapped by the auto-merge worker and the human merge handler to "recorded, not merged" (`shadow.would_have_merged`, no `RecordConfirmed`, excluded from §21.2 calibration); `shadow_egress_suppressed` timeline events | §30.2, §30.6, §30.7 |
| 98 | outbox classification + epoch stamps | `suppressed_in_shadow` stamped at enqueue in `OutboxStore.Create` (in-transaction, the single choke point — a born-shadow row is terminally shadow, whatever the flag says at delivery); a terminal mark written when the worker "delivers" a suppressed row into the ledger instead of the world (§30.6 — distinguishes a ledger-terminal row from a genuinely-delivered one for Step 104's UNION read model); exhaustive External/Internal classification of all 19 `NotificationKind`s inside `outboxworker.NewBuilder`, which receives the finished map and refuses to boot on an unclassified kind (the map is mutated after the `main.go` wiring line — preview kinds, `blob_delete` — so a wrap there would exempt later inserts); PASS-THROUGH mandatory for `blob_delete` (internal hygiene) and `sentinel_auto_fix` (the one hybrid: internal work runs, external write via the decorated port); `github_description_autofix` classified SUPPRESS, not a hybrid — its `Deliver` has no internal work of its own, every precondition it checks is a read (§30.2); `linear_digest` PASS-THROUGH (deliberate dead-letter, unchanged); delivery-time suppress-wins check for born-live rows crossing a demotion; egress-mode stamp on `review_verdicts` rows with query-level exclusion (`ListLatestAutoApproved`, re-trigger, digest — never call-site checks) + the promotion fence timestamp | §30.2, §30.8 |
| 99 ∥ | sandbox OS isolation (UID drop) | `supervisor.Spawn` grows a `Credential` in its `SysProcAttr` (today: `Setpgid` only) — the agent runtime runs under a different UID (user namespace where the substrate allows) and can no longer read `/proc/<sandbox-agent-pid>/environ` (sandbox bearer, SESSION_CONFIG — the `EnvWithout` strip is cosmetic today) or the credential cache (`/tmp/narvi-credentials`, whose 0600 is fictional against a same-UID shell); inventory-and-preserve pass over the runtime's legitimate surface (workspace tree, its own injected provider credential, the Step 70 loopback endpoint — loopback stays shared, the network namespace is not the boundary drawn); platform-wide hardening valid in live mode, closing the bearer-exfiltration half the read-only token does not cover — the codebase's own named debt (`scmcredentials.go` doc comment) | §30.5 |
| 100 | GitHub App read-only installation tokens | The other half of the same named debt: App id + private key in `platform.Config` (fail-fast validation), installation-token minting scoped `contents:read` + `metadata:read`, short-lived with auto-refresh; fail-closed scope introspection at boot and at mint (`X-OAuth-Scopes` / installation permissions — an operator pasting a broad classic credential is a loud refusal, never a silent re-arming); both halves of §30.6's credential recording: a mint refused by this scope check records the refusal into `shadow_scm_writes`, **and a served substitution records itself** — the success record is the load-bearing one, since a refusal is loud (403) while a substitution is invisible until something tries to push (same record-or-fail semantics and no-`Token`-field record types Step 97 establishes; one implementation in `internal/app/readonlymint`, called by the handler rather than copied into it); chosen over the fine-grained-PAT stopgap and the CP git-proxy fallback — a credential that physically cannot write is equally harmless in an env, a cache file, a baked image, or a restored snapshot. **Delivered alongside it, moved here from the next row because that is where the work actually landed**: the single server-side interception at the top of `ScmCredentials` covering BOTH mint branches (review→botToken — since replaced: a review session is read-only and receives the read-only token in every mode, §30.4 — and creator→OAuth) with its named test that a shadow review session receives a read-only credential; `forceReadOnly` threaded through the sandbox credential helper; the read-only mint forced for `BootModeBuild` with `CredentialCacheDir` purged in `CleanForImageBuild`; and the unconditional boot-time purge at the start of every `runBootSequence`, in all modes (still not load-bearing on its own — the control that would be, a purge at snapshot-**mint** time, remains the next row's). The rows say what shipped where rather than what was planned to | §30.4, §30.6 |
| 101 | sandbox shadow capability | **The residue the previous Step's substitution does not reach.** The interception, the build-path forcing and the helper threading landed with that Step (see its row); what remains is everything the credential can outlive: cache purge at snapshot **mint** time so no snapshot ever contains a token (`freshCacheHit` otherwise serves a restored write token with zero CP cooperation) + shadow bit on snapshot rows as fail-closed defense-in-depth (absent bit ⇒ treat as live ⇒ refuse); per-turn push/PR mode decision resolved at push send and persisted with the signal (`createPRBestEffort` honors it, never a re-read); demotion terminates/respawns all repo sandboxes and cancels in-flight push signals (the `ScmCredentialTTL` window, documented) | §30.4, §30.6, §30.8 |
| 102 | synchronous ingress seams + arch-test | Recording `CommentPoster` implementation (the interface at `planawaitingreply.go:45` already exists); single gated client per provider with narrow decorated mutation interfaces for Slack (ack + interactive) and Linear — Linear suppressed at the client-method level, never the transport (everything is `POST /graphql`); ingress packages lose the right to construct clients (`newAckClient` moves behind the injected seam); CI arch-test (depguard/forbidigo), two mechanisms: `os/exec` import banned outside `internal/adapters/outbound`/`internal/sandboxagent`/`cmd/sandbox-agent` (already clean, 0 files); `net/http` **client-side symbols** (`http.Client`, `http.DefaultClient`, `http.NewRequest(WithContext)`, `http.Get`/`Post`/`Head`, `http.Transport`) banned outside those same trees plus `cmd/control-plane`'s composition-root wiring — server-side `net/http` stays permitted everywhere; ratcheted from this Step's landing against a two-entry audited baseline (`inbound/auth/callback.go`'s GitHub OAuth identity reads; the pre-fix `slack/ack.go`+`handler.go` client this Step itself retires, dropping the baseline to one); carries the §30.9 chat-trigger decision (suppress acks and stay silent in the customer workspace, vs refuse/ignore chat-originated triggers in shadow), resolved before this Step ships. Mandatory the moment a customer's Slack or Linear is connected | §30.3, §30.9 |
| 103 | shadow lane coherence | **Both §30.9 decisions this row carried are resolved (see §30.9): no git mirror, and single-hop validation.** A per-session mirror would put complete customer repositories at rest on Narvi's own storage, in a capability whose retention policy is explicitly unresolved — and most of what it bought follows from not letting the push FAIL. So: the shadow-stamped push is **short-circuited at send** rather than attempted and failed, recording "would have pushed branch + would have opened PR" from the signal the turn already carries (§30.4 endorses this gate for consistency and state coherence, and denies only that it is security — Step 101 left it ungated deliberately and said so). Sentinel-fix lane stops **before** the one-shot claim, and before `createFixBranch` runs at all: one ledger entry, findings stay `open`, never `fix_pending`, which would also have killed manual apply-suggestion. Apply-suggestion answers "recorded, not committed" with a `fix_recorded` status that re-review ingestion treats as still-open — an update, never a contradiction. **No `github_pr_session` is synthesized**: exercising review-of-own-work, auto-approval, description-autofix and handoff internally would multiply the number of write paths running inside a capability whose bar is that one leaked PR is total failure, and the ledger says so where a reader lands | §30.7, §30.9 |
| 104 | shadow operator surface | **Location corrected from "Settings → Environments": the sentinel-autofix and auto-merge toggles this row places the card beside live in `web/src/session/RepoSettingsView.tsx`, and `EnvironmentsPanel.tsx` has none of them.** The repo-settings card, then, beside those two toggles: ledger summary — suppressed comments/PRs/pushes, the LLM-spend line (§30.1's surfaced-not-suppressed posture), links into sessions — and **Activate** as the graduation gesture, applying §30.8's promotion fence + shadow-era artifact quarantine (refuse or explicitly quarantine while unhandled shadow-era rows exist); the §30.9 ledger-visibility decision is **resolved: admin-only**, and affirmatively rather than by default — this ledger is the only surface holding customer source code at rest IN FULL (`UpdateFileContent.Content` is carried whole, because a digest does not answer the evaluator's question), and §30.9's own retention/null-out policy for that corpus is still open; deciding who may read a body of customer code before deciding how long it lives is the wrong order, so retention is the gate on ever widening to maintainer+; no §13.3 matrix row covers this (customer code at rest, beyond even the Settings → Members audit-log row's admin-only exposure); UNION read model over marked outbox rows + `shadow_scm_writes` (§16.2's "a read model, not new state"); heavy-content column split taken at schema time, retention/PII policy left as the §30.9 deferred decision (unresolved, unlike visibility above — the schema-time move ships regardless); extends Step 86's Settings surfaces | §30.6, §30.8 |

**Phase 8 milestone**: a dedicated evaluation deployment (`NARVI_SHADOW_MODE=1`, GitHub-only
webhooks, credential-starved per §30.4) attached to a live customer repository completes real
sessions end-to-end with zero customer-visible egress — reads only on the customer's audit
surface — and the suppression ledger accounts for every would-have-been effect; Activate
graduates a repo with the promotion fence applied. The minimal *safe* subset is Steps 96-101;
Step 102 becomes mandatory once a customer's Slack or Linear is connected; Steps 103-104 make the
evaluation good rather than merely safe.

## Phase 9 — Per-repository knowledge, two modes (6 Steps)

The two-mode per-repository knowledge base — mode A injects everything into the review prompt,
mode B maintains a per-repo documentary corpus in OKF form with embeddings and hybrid RAG
retrieval ranked within the same server-derived candidate gate mode A orders by recency
(gate-then-rank, §31.6 — hybrid retrieval improves recall, never poisoning resistance, §31.5),
strict per-repository isolation throughout (technical plan §31). On placement: by
content this is review-lane work and Phase 5 is its substrate (the three prompt seams, the
verdict schema, the §26.5 contestation instrument it measures on), but Phase 5's Steps were long
since scoped and shipped — folding six Steps into a closed phase would rewrite history, and
inserting them mid-sequence would shift Steps 72-104 (the Phase 4 standing rule) and buy nothing:
no earlier Step depends on any of these. It therefore lands appended as its own phase, the same
reasoning Phase 8's header gives for itself. Execution order: schedulable any time after Phase
5's milestone; it does not wait on Phases 6-7, and of Phase 8 it needs only Steps 96/97 (the
egress resolver and the verdict egress-mode stamp §31.8's in-query exclusions ride — Step 98 for
Step 107's SELECT, Step 96 for Step 108's corpus stamp) and Step 104's Activate surface (which
Step 108's graduation barrier extends). Two prerequisites are independent fixes already in
flight, dependencies of this phase but deliberately not Steps of it: the repository-authorization
fix on the four leaking handlers (`reviewanalytics.go`, `reposettings.go`,
`falsepositivepatterns.go`, `providercredentials.go` — §31.4) and G1's write-path sanitization of
the digest fields (§31.7). One decision gates whether Step 108 executes its content at all, not
merely when it starts, and is resolved there, never silently defaulted: Step 108 ships its full
mode B build only if Step 107's deterministic-arm baseline readout (§31.6 — the fork resolved as
build-the-selector-first, which defers the commitment to embeddings, not their construction)
engages the embeddings leg; if that readout kills engagement instead, Step 108's entire
deliverable is a recorded kill decision, and Steps 109-110 (both of which need its corpus tables)
never ship (§31.10).

| Step | Title | Content | Ref. |
|---|---|---|---|

Orthogonal prerequisite owed independently of the corpus; the irreversible pre-Step loss (plans whose sessions were already deleted) is recorded in §31.3, never backfillable. `reviewcontext.FetchPriorArchDecisions` (exact sibling of `FetchFalsePositivePatterns`) at all three seams (`github/handler.go`, `httpapi/reviewretrigger.go`, `sessionactor/reviewretrigger.go`) + pure sanitizing render with fixed delimiter (G2, `RenderAdvisoryBlock`'s mirror, pinned by the placeholder-drift-test precedent); recency fallback on empty overlap; mode B later re-ranks the same gated candidates, it never re-selects — the flip changes ordering and substrate, not eligibility); the mode buffer (§31.2): `turns.review_knowledge_mode` set once at creation (000080's rule verbatim), knowledge-mode stamp on `review_verdicts` (in-query attribution, 000081 precedent), **G5's knowledge-influenced stamp on `review_verdicts`** (any turn whose prompt carried a prior-decisions block, either mode — first-class state from the day injection starts, §31.7, so no unstamped-but-influenced verdict ever enters the gatable population Step 108 will ingest), injected-ids JSONB record on the turn (ids + content hashes + selector used + degradation, 000083's shape — durable joinable state, never logs);
